> <result>## Findings
> 
> ### (a) What a "seed card" is, and its schema — two distinct systems
> 
> There are **two separate "seed" concepts** in Venkat's ecosystem, at different maturity/scope levels. It's worth flagging this distinction clearly since both are called "seed cards":
> 
> **1. The old/heavy system — `my-os/techniques/` "Seed Card v1.2"**
> This is a rich, MCP-backed evidence artifact, not a lightweight note. Canonical schema doc: `/Users/venkatgullapalli/My_AI_Lab/new-projects/my-os/techniques/.claude/rules/references/seed-card-schema.md`, governed by `/Users/venkatgullapalli/My_AI_Lab/new-projects/my-os/techniques/.claude/rules/seed-card-storage.md`.
> 
> - **Definition**: a session-mined record capturing what happened, decisions, reasoning, evidence, and a single buyer-facing claim, produced by calling `mcp__cognitive-memory__store_seed_card`.
> - **Dual-axis grading** (required fields):
>   - `quality_grade` (A–F) — signal fidelity, computed by MCP validation pipeline (confidence × accuracy). Thresholds: A ≥0.80, B ≥0.60, C ≥0.40, D ≥0.20, F &lt;0.20 (per `seed-card-storage.md`).
>   - `strategic_grade` (A–D) — buyer relevance, derived from a 3-question buyer model (`strategic_buyer_problem` non-empty + `strategic_evidence_hop` 1/2/3-hop/no-path).
> - **"The Claim" (v1.2 rule)**: exactly 0 or 1 `the_claim` per card (one defensible, prescriptive, buyer-facing assertion) with `claim_confidence` (float, must be ≥0.75 for "content activation," alongside `quality_grade` ≥ B). If a session yields 2–3 angles, the card **splits into child cards** (`split_into`/`split_from` fields).
> - **Required cross-reference fields**: `seed_card_id`, `session_id`, `session_slug`, `session_file`, `session_date`, `child_topic_card_ids`.
> - **Body sections** (from example cards read in full): What Happened (Decomposed/Connected), Decisions (Made/Deferred), How We Thought, Reactions, Harvest (Content-Ready, Open Threads), Evidence (Numbers, Vocabulary coined, System Changes), Connections, Proof Artifacts table, Ontology table, Strategic Value, Claim.
> - Storage path: `docs/evidence/seed-cards/grade-{a,b,c,d,f}/{YYYY-MM-DD}--{project-slug}--{grade}--{title-slug}.md`. MCP writes to a flat staging area first; a copy must land at the canonical graded path (verified/copied per rule).
> 
> Example read in full: `2026-02-25--hybrid-rag-personal-os--a--frozen-config-staleness-enum-split-schem.md` (quality A 0.802, strategic B) and `2026-02-21--hybrid-rag-personal-os--b--technique-scope-discovery-from-broken-fo.md` (quality B 0.666, strategic B). Notably both of these two files sit at the **top level** of `docs/evidence/seed-cards/`, not filed into their grade subfolders — a filing inconsistency versus the stated storage rule.
> 
> **2. The new/light system — chief-of-staff `seeds/session/` (seed-capture skill)**
> Defined in `/Users/venkatgullapalli/Documents/my-ai-lab-v2/chief-of-staff/.claude/skills/seed-capture/SKILL.md`. This is deliberately minimal — explicitly **not** scored, not clustered, not promoted at capture time.
> 
> &gt; "A seed is one idea worth keeping: what it is, the tension it carries, where it came from, dated."
> 
> Required fields (template in SKILL.md):
> ```
> # &lt;The claim, as a full sentence — the title IS the claim&gt;
> - ID: &lt;PREFIX&gt;-&lt;NNN&gt;
> - Source: &lt;where it happened, with a path or pointer to the record&gt;
> - Date: &lt;YYYY-MM-DD of the moment, not of the writing&gt;
> - Status: seedling
> - Attribution: &lt;his | endorsed | other | system&gt; — &lt;one line of who/how&gt;
> - Tension: &lt;the friction that makes it worth keeping&gt;
> ## The claim
> ## Why it matters
> ## Connections
> ```
> - **Attribution class is mandatory**: his / endorsed / other / system.
> - **No quality scores at capture** — "Facts stored; judgments computed when a decision needs one."
> - **Triage threshold** (seed A-LIVE-024): pass 3 of 4 — repeated (3+ occurrences or named one-off), his (not borrowed), the label carries the rule, someone would ask for it by name. Base rate: 0–3 seeds/session; zero is valid and expected; &gt;5 triggers a stop-and-ask.
> - **Naming is still pending** — this is explicitly a placeholder system (see (d) below).
> 
> ### (b) Where seeds are stored, and how grading works
> 
> - **Old system**: `docs/evidence/seed-cards/grade-{a,b,c,d,f}/` under `my-os/techniques/`, with quality grade computed by the MCP `store_seed_card` tool (confidence × accuracy) and strategic grade computed separately by a 3-question buyer-relevance model. The two axes are explicitly independent ("A card can be quality A + strategic D").
> - **New system**: `seeds/session/` under `chief-of-staff/`, with only a running count tracked in `seeds/README.md` — no grading at capture. Grading/scoring/cultivation is deferred to a "weekly review" cadence (per seed A-LIVE-023) and is explicitly out of scope for the capture skill.
> - `docs/evidence/seed-cards/grade-b/` (79 files) is the largest bucket, containing session-mined seeds across many projects (e.g., `personal-ai-os`, `hybrid-rag-personal-os`, `linkedin-profile-optimizer`, `namika`, `cognitive-memory-mcp`). `topic-cards/grade-a/` (4 files) sits downstream of seed cards.
> 
> ### (c) "Abductive extension" — full explanation with quotes
> 
> Found only in `/Users/venkatgullapalli/Documents/my-ai-lab-v2/chief-of-staff/reference/conversations/chatgpt-chief-of-staff-os-advisor/Chief of Staff Advisor - Phase 2.5 Review Analysis.md` (and its two verbatim `.txt` export duplicates). Not present anywhere else in either repo, and not (yet) in `memory/concepts.md`.
> 
> This term comes out of a ChatGPT-authored conceptual discussion (a "Chief of Staff OS Advisor" conversation), not a code artifact. Sequence of how it's built up:
> 
> 1. First framed as a **directional reasoning principle** (line 3442):
> &gt; "Preserve the existing experiment, identify a question it cannot answer, then add the smallest independent perspective needed to answer that question."
> 
> 2. Named provisionally as **"non-destructive expansion"** or **"orthogonal extension under uncertainty"** (lines 3450–3454):
> &gt; "When the existing framing is useful and internally coherent, do not replace it merely because another perspective exists. Look for dimensions the current framing cannot observe. Add a complementary lens only when it reveals information that would otherwise remain unavailable."
> &gt; Directional posture: **"preserve what is working → find the blind spot → add an independent view → compare → learn"**
> 
> 3. Sharpened into the compact principle (line 3522):
> &gt; "**Add the smallest independent perspective that resolves a material uncertainty the existing framing cannot resolve by itself.**"
> 
> 4. Then explicitly given the name **"abductive extension"** (lines 3578–3602), distinguishing it from optimization:
> &gt; "Optimization stays inside the existing objective and asks: 'How do we make this better?' This move questions the observational boundary: 'Are we even looking at the problem from enough independent directions to know what "better" means?'"
> &gt; "**Another useful name is abductive extension.** Abduction means generating a plausible explanation or possibility that accounts for something the current frame does not fully explain."
> &gt; Triggers: "a repeated frustration, an unexpected result, a contradiction, an analogy, a correction, a capability that seems useful somewhere else, two ideas that share a deeper mechanism." The reasoning partner notices "There may be another thing going on here" and "introduces it as something worth testing" rather than declaring it correct immediately — "That distinction is important."
> 
> 5. A 6-step pattern is proposed for mining conversation history for this behavior (lines 3608–3626): Existing frame → Latent limitation → Model-generated extension → Preservation → New information → User uptake.
> 
> 6. Compressed principle (lines 3632, 3636):
> &gt; "Treat a good framing as a platform for further discovery, not as a boundary around the problem. Preserve what it already explains, then search for important questions it leaves unanswerable."
> &gt; "Do not replace a useful model when an adjacent model can expose what the first one cannot see."
> 
> 7. Venkat then **applies it operationally** to his own Phase 2.5 audit process (line 3652), asking ChatGPT to apply "the abductive extension" to a stalled, repeatedly-revised audit. The response (lines 3659–3721) proposes adding one bounded "Audit Sufficiency Challenge" rather than another audit pass, and derives a broader OS rule (line 3748–3754):
> &gt; "When repeated correction continues to improve a process, ask whether the remaining uncertainty belongs to the model of the problem or to reality itself. If it belongs to the model, keep reasoning. If it belongs to reality, go encounter reality."
> 
> 8. Finally, when the conversation produces a **v3.1 Advisor Charter draft**, the concept is formally folded in under the name **"Non-destructive extension"** rather than "abductive extension" (line 3859):
> &gt; "Added **Non-destructive extension** as a reasoning principle: preserve a useful framing and add the smallest independent perspective only when it resolves a material uncertainty the current frame does not."
> 
> So in this system: "abductive extension" and "non-destructive extension" are used as near-synonyms for the same reasoning move — the more polished/adopted operational name in the actual charter draft is "non-destructive extension," while "abductive extension" is the more evocative label used mid-conversation to name the abduction-flavored version of the same idea (generating a plausible unexplained-cause hypothesis, then testing rather than asserting it). It has not (as of this search) been formalized as a labeled concept in `memory/concepts.md` in the chief-of-staff repo, nor does it appear wired into the seed-capture skill or seed-card schema — it lives purely in this one reference conversation transcript.
> 
> ### (d) How the seed-capture skill works
> 
> File: `/Users/venkatgullapalli/Documents/my-ai-lab-v2/chief-of-staff/.claude/skills/seed-capture/SKILL.md`.
> 
> - **Trigger**: runs automatically as part of every `session-receipt` (standing consent recorded in the seed-capture-skill plan, approved by Venkat 2026-08-14); also triggerable mid-session on "capture this as a seed," "add this to the greenhouse," or "is this a seed?"
> - **Scope guard**: capture only — "No status moves, no composting, no scoring, no promotion." Writes only to `seeds/session/` and the count line in `seeds/README.md`, nothing else.
> - **Input**: the session's own transcript/context, swept for 4 candidate kinds — something he said, a phrasing he adopted, something a third party said about him, or an observed behavioral pattern with evidence.
> - **Triage**: seed A-LIVE-024 threshold, pass 3 of 4 (repeated/named, his, label-carries-the-rule, would-be-asked-for-by-name); zero is a valid outcome; &gt;5 stops and asks Venkat.
> - **Output**: one markdown file per surviving seed in `seeds/session/`, per the template in section (a) above, with an immutable zero-padded sequential ID and mandatory attribution class.
> - **Storage location**: `seeds/session/` (folder/prefix names explicitly flagged as **placeholders** pending Venkat's own naming — tracked as open loop #43 in the receipt below).
> - **Side effects**: updates the session count in `seeds/README.md`; asks once per run "did anything get a label today?" — new labels get one sentence into `memory/concepts.md`, or `Unknown` if no crisp definition exists (never guessed).
> - **Rules**: every written file must be verified to exist before being reported (evidence rule); Date = when the moment happened, not when written; quotes labeled "his" must be checkable against a record, and near-verbatim voice-transcription quotes are flagged as such; no secrets/restricted client content/financial figures; anything build-shaped still routes to `PLAN.md → Deferred`, not into a seed.
> 
> **Build/history context** (from receipt `state/receipts/2026-08-13-1025-seed-capture.md` and commit `f70bf2c` "...seed-capture built"): the skill emerged from a session where 489 candidate seeds (186 topic cards, 153 seed cards after dedup, 77 chronicle moments, 43 decision records, plus 30 live voicenote-derived seeds) were extracted into a session scratchpad but were **not durable** — that gap ("the 489 seeds have no durable home") became the motivating open loop. Four explicit rulings shaped the eventual skill design:
> 1. **One seed per artifact, no clustering at capture** — "Each one should be a seed." Merging pre-decides meaning; connections are recorded, clusters compute later.
> 2. **No batch scoring** — "Store facts, compute judgments" [when a decision needs one].
> 3. **Own naming, not borrowed** (not MINT/GREENHOUSE terminology) — Venkat wants his own system built from his own ground (ties to decision D-006).
> 4. Two premature runs (a full extraction pipeline and a clustered seed capture) were stopped mid-flight for contradicting these rulings and wrote nothing.
> 
> **Comparison — topic cards vs. seed cards** (`my-os/techniques`): topic cards (`topic-cards/grade-a/`) are downstream, second-generation artifacts derived *from* a seed card (`source_seed_card_id`/`source_seed_card_file` fields link back), reformatted around a single `claim_type` (e.g., "pattern") with a `## Receipts` table of individually-sourced, confidence-scored evidence rows, `## Counterpoints &amp; Gaps`, and (in the example read) an added "AI Participation Surface" interpretive layer. Where a seed card captures a whole session's raw happenings/decisions/evidence, a topic card is a single-claim, receipt-backed, content-ready distillation of one angle pulled out of a seed card.</result>

> **What the content actually is:** This is the **output directory of a formal, phased, evidence-based self-audit of the entire `chief-of-staff` repository**, executed per the methodology in `audit-kit/` (see below). It is not conversation transcript — it is structured audit deliverables: phase reports (markdown, heavily self-citing with evidence labels like `Confirmed`/`Provided`/`Observed`/`Inferred`) and machine-parseable registers (CSV: inventory, findings, dependencies, duplicates, recommendations, governance rules).
>

> - **Phase 2** (`phase-2-findings*.md` + `findings*.csv`, `dependency-map*.csv`) — 42 evidence-graded findings across six domains (structure, dependencies, governance, evidence custody, write-safety, etc.), e.g. no git remote/backup, concurrent-write data loss risk, undifferentiated ownership in `context/`/`state/`/`seeds/`.
> - **Phase 2.5** (`phase-2.5-outcome-and-application-discovery*.md/csv`) — AI-native outcome/application discovery.
> - **Phase 3** (`phase-3-target-and-gap*.md` + `gap-analysis*.csv`) — target-state requirements and gap analysis, run through a three-pass anti-anchoring protocol (independent derivation → advisor challenge → historical comparison); Venkat-confirmed.
> - **Phase 4** (`phase-4-recommendations*.md` + `recommendations*.csv`) — 23-row recommendation register (8 decision packages requiring Venkat's ruling, 9 implementations, 6 preserve/no-action calls), force-ranked to a Critical+High cap of 10.
> - **Phase 5** (`phase-5-governance-automation-health.md`, `governance-standard.md`, `governance-rule-register.csv` — 81 rules, `automation-opportunities.csv`, `health-baseline.csv`).
> - **`completeness/`** — a later, more elaborate re-qualification/"frontier innovation" sub-audit (dated Aug 16) with its own multi-pass structure: baseline, full repository census (1,200 files censused), blind evaluation packets, adversarial qualification, disconfirmation passes, cross-reference/graph-completeness checks, an incident register, and its own `scripts/` (Python: census-building, blind-packet construction, graph diagnostics, CSV validation).
> - **`scripts/`** at audit root — 4 Python scripts (`build_inventory.py`, `find_dupes.py`, `phase1_stats.py`, `scan_deps.py`) making Phase 0/1 evidence collection reproducible.
> 
> **Sample topic list:** repository purpose/classification; structural findings (undifferentiated ownership, no `reference/README.md`, empty dirs); dependency mapping and duplicate detection; evidence-custody/write-safety risk (data loss precedent, no off-machine backup — later resolved via `tmutil` check); AI-native innovation vs. redesign vs. automation classification; target-state requirements and gap grading; a 23-item recommendation register with decision packages (canonical operating-model home, write-safety fix, exclusion-list custody for outreach); governance rule register (81 rules); a full repository census with cluster inventory by size (seed cards 528 files/1.6MB, work-inventory 12.2MB, creator-research corpora 79.9MB, this very ChatGPT-advisor corpus 52MB).

> `audit-kit/` is the **methodology/prompt-pack that the `audit/` outputs were generated from** — not audit results themselves, but the reusable instrument. It's a 14-file, 208 KB set: `00-charter.md` (the shared evidence rules, calibration floor, goal-alignment gate, and prohibited-reasoning list every phase must obey), one prompt file per phase (`phase-0-baseline.md` through `phase-6-roadmap-and-final-audit.md`, plus `optional-skill-blueprint.md`), a `coverage-map.md`, and a fully-written `README.md` explaining why the original single 24-section audit prompt was split into seven phases chained through files (not conversation context) so each phase gets full model attention and a human checkpoint, with explicit model-tier guidance (Fable 5/Sonnet/Opus per phase) and enforcement mechanisms (evidence citation requirement, 60% calibration floor, 12-item Critical/High recommendation cap, preserve-before-changing default). > <ide_selection>The user selected the lines 1 to 134 from /Users/venkatgullapalli/.claude/plans/can-you-create-a-velvety-duckling.md:
> # Seed Mining Plan — ChatGPT Advisor Corpus + Audit Folder
> 
> **Status:** Draft — awaiting approval
> **Scope (his, mid-planning):** this repo only. All context — seed rules, positioning, goals — comes from `chief-of-staff/`. Nothing from `my-os/techniques` is used or referenced.
> **His three calls (2026-08-16):**
> - **What to mine for:** his thinking, first. His prompts, how his reasoning and judgment steered each conversation, the choices he made, and the outcomes his steering produced. Patterns in how he thinks are seed candidates in their own right. Ideas from the content are secondary and always carry honest attribution.
> - **Landing:** review file only. No seed files are written in this effort. Writing seeds is a later step he approves separately.
> - **Filter:** wide net. Anything with real tension makes the candidate list; he cuts at review.
> **On approval, first step:** save this plan to `plans/seed-mining-advisor-audit/seed-mining-advisor-audit--pipeline--extraction--v1--2026-08-16-<HHmm>.md` with full frontmatter, and add the row to `plans/INDEX.md` (per the plan-persistence rule and hard rule 16).
> 
> ---
> 
> ## The question this plan answers
> 
> Two questions, in priority order:
> 
> 1. **What do these records show about how Venkat thinks?** His prompts, his pushback, what he accepted, what he rejected, how his steering changed the outcome — and the repeatable patterns in that. Each named pattern with evidence is a seed candidate.
> 2. **What ideas worth keeping are in the content itself**, with honest attribution for who authored them?
> 
> The two unmined stores:
> 
> 1. `reference/conversations/chatgpt-chief-of-staff-os-advisor/` — 79 files, ~727k words, heavily duplicated. The ChatGPT advisor's charters, full transcripts, phase prompts, and strategy docs.
> 2. `audit/` — 99 files, ~194k words of markdown plus CSVs. The phased self-audit of this repo, including the Aug 16 completeness sub-audit.
> 
> ## Why it matters now
> 
> - The greenhouse already holds seeds from sessions and the audit interview (loop #50). These two folders are the largest unmined stores left.
> - The Aug 16 audit found the ChatGPT advisor's ideas already shaped the money plan without being tracked. Mining with clean attribution makes that influence visible instead of invisible.
> - Loop #60 (his ruling on the advisor boundary) is still open. Capturing with attribution labels does not pre-empt that ruling — it feeds it.
> 
> ## What "seed" means here
> 
> The seed-capture format from `.claude/skills/seed-capture/SKILL.md`:
> - One file per seed. The title is the claim, as a full sentence.
> - Fields: ID, Source (path + pointer), Date (of the moment), Status: seedling, Attribution (his / endorsed / other / system), Tension.
> - Sections: The claim / Why it matters / Connections.
> - Triage test (seed A-LIVE-024), 4 checks: repeated or named as a one-off; his (or clearly labeled if not); the label carries the rule; someone would ask for it by name. Under his wide-net call these are **sorting tags on the review list, not a kill filter** — he cuts at review.
> - No scoring, no clustering, no promotion at capture. Names of clients, employers, and brands stripped from title and body.
> 
> ## What "abductive extension" means here, and how it's applied
> 
> Defined in the corpus itself (`Chief of Staff Advisor - Phase 2.5 Review Analysis.md`):
> 
> > "Add the smallest independent perspective that resolves a material uncertainty the existing framing cannot resolve by itself."
> 
> Applied two ways during scanning:
> 1. **As a lens on the material.** For each strong frame found in the corpus, ask: what can this frame not see? Moments where the conversation itself did this move — noticed a blind spot and added a view — are prime seed candidates.
> 2. **As a lens on Venkat's positioning and goals — from this repo only.** For each candidate seed, ask: does this extend his current frames — "applied AI systems builder grounded in customer intelligence" (D-003), money-now as priority #1 (NOW.md), the five roots (ROOT.md), his definition of done (DONE.md) — by showing something those frames can't see on their own? A seed that extends a live frame ranks above one that just restates it.
> 
> ## Three approaches considered (rule 17)
> 
> ### Option A — One reader, chronological
> Read everything in order, one pass, one context. Extract seeds as they appear.
> - **Pros:** Best narrative fidelity. Sees how ideas evolved across conversations. No merge step.
> - **Cons:** ~900k words does not fit one context. Would take many sequential sessions with heavy compaction, losing exactly the cross-conversation memory that is its selling point. Slowest and most expensive.
> - **Strongest case for it:** the corpus is conversational, and arcs (like abductive extension being born, named, and adopted) only show up when you read in sequence.
> 
> ### Option B — Dedup, then parallel lane sweep, then skeptic (chosen)
> First strip duplicates and rank files. Then parallel extraction agents, each with the seed rubric and a positioning brief, sweep assigned files. A merge step dedups candidates. A separate skeptic pass tries to kill each candidate before it becomes a seed.
> - **Pros:** Covers everything unique. Scales to the volume. The skeptic pass is the disproof mechanism rule 16 asks for. Attribution is decided per candidate against the actual transcript turn.
> - **Cons:** Parallel readers can miss cross-file arcs. Mitigation: one dedicated "arc lane" reads the transcript files in date order looking only for multi-conversation arcs.
> - **Strongest case against it:** it costs more than Option C and some of that spend goes to files that yield nothing.
> 
> ### Option C — Frame-first targeted mine
> Start from his live frames (positioning anchor, money plan, seed system, audit method). Search the corpus only for passages that touch those frames. Mine only those files.
> - **Pros:** Cheapest. Every seed is relevant by construction.
> - **Cons:** Only finds what extends frames we already named. That is the opposite of the abductive move — the best seeds are the ones current frames can't see, and a frame-first search never looks there. High miss risk in a corpus this size.
> 
> ### Why B wins
> - A can't hold the volume. C structurally can't find the unknown-unknowns, which is what abductive extension is for.
> - B keeps A's strength through the dedicated arc lane and keeps C's strength by giving every extraction agent the positioning brief — relevance is scored, not used as a filter.
> - What B trades away: cost. Roughly 8–12 subagent runs. If a cheaper pass is wanted, C is the fallback, accepted as a partial mine.
> 
> ## Steps
> 
> **Step 0 — Persist this plan.** Copy to `plans/seed-mining-advisor-audit/` with frontmatter; add `plans/INDEX.md` row.
> 
> **Step 1 — Dedup and rank (script + one Sonnet agent).**
> - Hash all 79 conversation files; list byte-identical duplicates (many `(1)`/`(2)` copies). Duplicates are noted, never deleted (archive-only rule; they stay where they are).
> - Rank unique files into: high-yield (transcripts with "You:" turns, unique strategy docs), medium (charters, phase prompts), low (exports of things already in the repo).
> - Same for `audit/`: markdown reports in; CSVs sampled only where a report cites them. `audit-kit/` excluded unless he says otherwise.
> - Output: `<scratchpad>/mining-manifest.md` — every file, its bucket, its assigned lane.
> 
> **Step 2 — Parallel extraction lanes (Sonnet agents, ~5–8).**
> Each lane gets: its file list, the seed rubric, the abductive-extension lens, and a one-page context brief built only from this repo: D-003 identity, NOW.md priorities, ROOT.md roots, TASTE.md, the relevant open loops.
> 
> What every lane looks for, in priority order:
> 1. **His reasoning in the "You:" turns.** How he frames a problem, what he challenges, the questions he asks, the corrections he makes, the standards he holds work to. A pattern that shows up more than once gets named as a candidate with its occurrences listed.
> 2. **Steering-to-outcome links.** Places where his prompt or pushback visibly changed what the conversation produced — evidence of judgment, not just participation.
> 3. **Ideas in the content**, labeled honestly: his / endorsed (he adopted it) / other (ChatGPT-authored) / system (observed pattern).
> 4. **Build-shaped ideas** — listed separately for `PLAN.md → Deferred`, never as seeds.
> 
> Each lane returns candidate rows: claim sentence · source file + location · date · attribution · tension · which live frame it extends or challenges · verbatim supporting quote(s).
> - Lane split: advisor transcripts (2–3 lanes by date range) · strategy/positioning docs · phase prompts + charters · audit phase reports (his checkpoint rulings and confirmations are judgment evidence) · completeness sub-audit.
> - **Arc lane (1 agent):** reads transcripts in date order, hunting only cross-conversation arcs — how his thinking evolved, an idea born, tested, adopted, or dropped across files.
> 
> **Step 3 — Merge and skeptic pass (1–2 agents, frontier model).**
> - Merge candidates, dedup near-identical claims (record connections instead of clustering — his ruling).
> - Wide net means the skeptic verifies, it does not cut for taste: Is the quote real and in context? Is attribution honest (his words vs. ChatGPT's)? Does it collide with a constraint below? Only fabrication, wrong attribution, or a constraint hit removes a candidate. Each survivor gets triage tags (which of the 4 tests it passes) so he can sort at review, plus a one-line skeptic note.
> 
> **Step 4 — The deliverable: one review file. The effort ends here.**
> - Every surviving candidate as: claim · attribution · source pointer · the frame it extends · triage tags · skeptic note. Bulleted, plain words, grouped: his thinking patterns first, then endorsed ideas, then other-authored ideas, then build-shaped items headed for `PLAN.md → Deferred`.
> - **No seed files are written.** Converting approved candidates into seeds is a separate later step he approves after reading the list.
> - Receipt + NOW.md + open-loops entry written at close (the unreviewed list is itself an open loop).
> 
> ## Hard constraints carried into every lane
> 
> - Names stripped: no client, employer, or brand names in any seed title or body (`seeds/README.md` rule).
> - D-003: "data strategy consultant" never surfaces as current identity, even if the corpus says it.
> - Loop #51: no RDEB / patient-count numbers in any seed until resolved.
> - Loop #48: nothing from the archived post-doc-16 transcript material, even if the corpus quotes it.
> - Attribution honesty: ChatGPT-authored ideas are "other" unless the transcript shows Venkat adopting them ("endorsed"). His own "You:" turns are "his." Abductive extension itself, as a concept, is ChatGPT-authored — it gets captured with that label, not laundered into "his."
> - Pending #60: seeds from the advisor corpus carry a note that the advisor-boundary ruling is open; capture is compatible, use is his later call.
> - Nothing build-shaped becomes a seed — build ideas found in the corpus route to `PLAN.md → Deferred` (skill rule), listed separately in the Step 4 review file.
> 
> ## What would disprove the effort
> 
> - The skeptic kills most candidates on verification → the corpus is thinner than it looks; report that honestly, don't pad.
> - His "You:" turns yield few repeatable patterns → the mine mostly documents ChatGPT's thinking, not his; that itself is a finding feeding loop #60.
> - Zero candidates is a valid outcome (skill rule).
> 
> ## Success criteria
> 
> - Every unique file in both folders is in the manifest with a lane and a disposition (mined / sampled / skipped, with reason).
> - The review file leads with his thinking patterns, each backed by real quotes from more than one place where the pattern claims repetition.
> - Every candidate has honest attribution and a source pointer that resolves.
> - Nothing written to `seeds/` in this effort. Receipt + NOW.md + open loop recorded at close.
> 
> ## Risks
> 
> - **Volume risk:** even deduped, the corpus is big; lanes may need splitting mid-run. Manifest makes that visible.
> - **Priority risk:** money is priority #1 (NOW.md). This is "slow system" work. The plan runs when he schedules it, not by default ahead of outreach/taxes.
> - **Positioning-conflict risk:** parts of the corpus use consulting framings that D-003 superseded; the context brief keeps D-003 as the ruling frame and marks conflicts instead of resolving them.
> 
> 
> This may or may not be related to the current task.</ide_selection>
> # Claude Code Execution Prompt: Seed Mining Calibration + Pipeline Revision
> 
> ## Role
> 
> You are working inside Venkat Gullapalli's Chief of Staff OS repository.
> 
> Your task is to revise the existing seed-mining plan and execute the smallest bounded calibration run needed to validate the mining method before processing the full ChatGPT Advisor and audit corpus.
> 
> This is evidence recovery work.
> 
> Do not turn the exercise into content generation, architecture design, positioning work, or a broad audit.
> 
> The primary object of study is Venkat's thinking as expressed through real conversations and decisions.
> 
> ---
> 
> # Goal
> 
> Build and test a seed-mining method that answers, in priority order:
> 
> 1. What do these records reveal about how Venkat thinks, reasons, questions, challenges, corrects, decides, changes his mind, and steers work?
> 2. What reusable ideas, tensions, stories, questions, methods, application hypotheses, and other intellectual material exist in the corpus?
> 3. Which apparent repetitions represent independent evidence, and which are merely propagation of the same original idea through later documents?
> 4. Which recovered items deserve review as possible seeds, without deciding yet whether they become seeds.
> 
> The output of this execution is a calibrated mining method and a reviewable calibration sample.
> 
> Do not run the full corpus mine yet.
> 
> ---
> 
> # Governing principle
> 
> Separate recovery from interpretation.
> 
> Use this sequence:
> 
> `recover independently -> establish lineage -> reconstruct arcs -> interpret against current context -> verify -> present for Venkat review`
> 
> Do not let today's positioning, audit conclusions, Advisor language, ROOT principles, or current priorities determine what gets noticed during initial extraction.
> 
> Current framing enters only after independent recovery.
> 
> ---
> 
> # Repository scope
> 
> Work only inside the Chief of Staff repository.
> 
> Primary mining stores:
> 
> 1. `reference/conversations/chatgpt-chief-of-staff-os-advisor/`
> 2. `audit/`
> 
> Do not use material from `my-os/techniques`.
> 
> Do not import external research.
> 
> Do not browse the web.
> 
> Do not treat project conversation history as canon.
> 
> Repository canon outranks reasoning history.
> 
> ---
> 
> # Governing sources
> 
> Before changing or executing the plan, read the relevant current repository files, including at minimum:
> 
> * `DECISIONS.md`
> * `DONE.md`
> * `ROOT.md`
> * `VISION.md`
> * `TASTE.md`
> * `NOW.md`, if present
> * `PLAN.md`, if present
> * `.claude/skills/seed-capture/SKILL.md`
> * `CHIEF-OF-STAFF-OS-ADVISOR-v3.1-DRAFT.md`, wherever the current repo copy resides
> * the existing draft seed-mining plan supplied for this work
> 
> Use them for constraints, governance, later interpretation, and validation.
> 
> Do not use current positioning or these governing frames to guide the first recovery pass except where required for hard constraints such as privacy, attribution, prohibited material, or repository governance.
> 
> ---
> 
> # Existing plan
> 
> The existing draft is titled:
> 
> `Seed Mining Plan — ChatGPT Advisor Corpus + Audit Folder`
> 
> Preserve its central decisions:
> 
> * Venkat's thinking is the primary object of mining.
> * Content ideas are secondary.
> * Attribution stays explicit.
> * Use a wide net.
> * The deliverable is a review file.
> * Do not write seed files during this effort.
> * Venkat decides what becomes a seed.
> * Cross-conversation arcs matter.
> * Zero useful candidates is a valid result.
> * Names and restricted material remain protected.
> * Build implementation instructions do not automatically become seeds.
> 
> Revise the plan where needed to incorporate the requirements below.
> 
> Do not overwrite or silently mutate an already persisted frozen plan if one exists.
> 
> If an earlier persisted version exists, preserve it and create a new version.
> 
> ---
> 
> # Required changes to the mining design
> 
> ## 1. Split recovery from interpretation
> 
> The first extraction pass must be context-light.
> 
> ### Recovery pass receives
> 
> * source material
> * neutral seed-candidate definition
> * attribution rules
> * privacy and restricted-material rules
> * source-pointer requirements
> * lineage requirements
> * instructions to preserve uncertainty
> 
> ### Recovery pass does NOT receive as discovery lenses
> 
> * current positioning
> * D-003 identity framing
> * revenue priorities
> * ROOT as a list of things to search for
> * current audit findings
> * Public Application Discovery framing
> * Advisor-derived concepts
> * abductive extension
> * non-destructive extension
> * current target-state concepts
> * any instruction asking which current frame the candidate supports
> 
> The recovery pass should look broadly for:
> 
> * reasoning moves
> * questions
> * judgments
> * decisions
> * corrections
> * pushback
> * rejected assumptions
> * accepted assumptions
> * changes of mind
> * unresolved tensions
> * stories
> * analogies
> * distinctions
> * claims
> * methods
> * patterns
> * hypotheses
> * counterexamples
> * failures
> * lessons
> * steering moments
> * applications
> * unusual observations
> * ideas worth preserving
> * moments where Venkat changed the direction or quality of the output
> 
> The test is:
> 
> > Is something intellectually, behaviorally, or strategically interesting happening here that might deserve later review?
> 
> Do not ask yet whether it fits current positioning.
> 
> ---
> 
> ## 2. Add episode-level lineage
> 
> File count is not evidence count.
> 
> The same original statement might later appear in:
> 
> * another assistant response
> * an audit report
> * a phase prompt
> * a charter
> * a strategy document
> * another transcript export
> 
> Do not treat these as independent repetitions.
> 
> For every candidate, track where feasible:
> 
> * `source_file`
> * `source_location`
> * `source_date`
> * `speaker`
> * `origin_episode_id`
> * `origin_type`
> * `derived_from`
> * `lineage_status`
> * `independent_episode_count`
> 
> Suggested `lineage_status` values:
> 
> * `origin`
> * `independent_recurrence`
> * `later_reinforcement`
> * `derived_restatement`
> * `quoted_copy`
> * `uncertain_lineage`
> 
> The evidence unit for a recurring thinking pattern is the distinct judgment episode, not the number of files containing similar language.
> 
> ---
> 
> ## 3. Deduplicate below the file level
> 
> Whole-file hashes are necessary but insufficient.
> 
> Detect:
> 
> * exact duplicate files
> * lightly modified duplicate exports
> * duplicated transcript turns
> * transcript excerpts embedded in audit files
> * repeated prompts
> * copied assistant outputs
> * overlapping conversation ranges
> * documents derived from earlier conversations
> 
> Do not delete anything.
> 
> Produce lineage metadata sufficient to distinguish:
> 
> > another independent occurrence
> 
> from
> 
> > another copy or downstream restatement of the same occurrence.
> 
> Use deterministic scripts where text fingerprinting, hashing, similarity checks, or stable parsing is enough.
> 
> Use model judgment only where semantic comparison is needed.
> 
> Preserve inspectable outputs.
> 
> ---
> 
> ## 4. Make reasoning transitions first-class candidates
> 
> Do not mine only static beliefs or repeated patterns.
> 
> Look specifically for transitions such as:
> 
> `initial framing -> challenge -> correction -> stronger framing`
> 
> `hypothesis -> counterexample -> rejection`
> 
> `idea -> pressure test -> adoption`
> 
> `concept -> split into two distinct concepts`
> 
> `assumption -> contradiction -> changed rule`
> 
> `recommendation -> Venkat pushback -> materially different outcome`
> 
> `uncertainty -> new distinction -> decision`
> 
> A transition itself may be more valuable than the final claim.
> 
> Capture both:
> 
> * what changed
> * why it changed
> 
> Where repetition is claimed, support it with independent episodes rather than derivative repetition.
> 
> ---
> 
> ## 5. Distinguish implementation from underlying insight
> 
> Replace any absolute rule saying build-shaped ideas are never seeds.
> 
> Use this rule:
> 
> > Implementation instructions are not seeds. When a build-shaped item contains an underlying claim, tension, reasoning principle, reusable insight, application hypothesis, or conceptual distinction, extract the conceptual layer separately. Route the implementation layer to `PLAN.md -> Deferred` or the appropriate existing planning location only if the governing workflow calls for that routing.
> 
> Example:
> 
> Implementation:
> 
> > Build a graph-backed reasoning registry.
> 
> Possible conceptual candidate:
> 
> > Repeated ideas need to be distinguished from ideas that merely propagated through derivative documents.
> 
> Keep those two objects separate.
> 
> Do not convert implementation wishes into conceptual insight unless the source supports the abstraction.
> 
> ---
> 
> ## 6. Do not use abductive extension as an extraction lens
> 
> Do not instruct recovery agents to search through the corpus using "abductive extension" or any other Advisor-derived framework as the organizing lens.
> 
> Such concepts may themselves appear as candidates with honest attribution.
> 
> They may also be used later during interpretation.
> 
> They must not shape initial recovery.
> 
> ---
> 
> ## 7. Add possible-use tags only after recovery
> 
> During interpretation, each candidate may receive zero or more provisional possible-use tags:
> 
> * `publishing`
> * `story`
> * `positioning`
> * `public_application`
> * `research`
> * `revenue`
> * `operating_insight`
> * `procedural_memory`
> * `decision_instrument`
> * `system_design`
> * `unknown`
> 
> These are routing hints, not promotion decisions.
> 
> A candidate does not need a current use to survive review.
> 
> `unknown` is acceptable.
> 
> Do not force every candidate into publishing or Public Application Discovery.
> 
> ---
> 
> # Attribution rules
> 
> Use explicit attribution.
> 
> At minimum:
> 
> * `his`: originated in Venkat's words or reasoning
> * `endorsed`: originated elsewhere but Venkat explicitly adopted, reformulated, or approved the substance
> * `other`: assistant or other-source idea without evidence of adoption
> * `system`: pattern inferred from multiple episodes rather than stated directly by Venkat
> 
> Do not launder assistant-originated concepts into Venkat's thinking.
> 
> If origin is uncertain, mark attribution uncertain.
> 
> Venkat's later adoption is evidence of endorsement, not proof he originated the idea.
> 
> ---
> 
> # Evidence classes
> 
> For every material candidate distinguish:
> 
> * Observed
> * Provided
> * Supported inference
> * Unknown
> * Hypothesis
> 
> Do not silently promote inference to fact.
> 
> ---
> 
> # Calibration tranche
> 
> Before mining the full corpus, select a deliberately varied calibration set.
> 
> Choose approximately five source items representing:
> 
> 1. one early substantive Advisor conversation
> 2. one recent substantive Advisor conversation
> 3. one conversation containing strong Venkat pushback or correction
> 4. one audit artifact containing Venkat checkpoint rulings, confirmations, or judgment evidence
> 5. one strategy, positioning, or planning artifact materially derived from conversation history
> 
> If overlap makes one source satisfy two categories, select another source so the tranche still contains distinct material.
> 
> Document why each calibration source was selected.
> 
> Do not cherry-pick only obviously rich sources.
> 
> ---
> 
> # Calibration execution
> 
> Run the calibration in separate stages.
> 
> ## Stage A: Independent recovery
> 
> Use fresh extraction contexts if the available orchestration supports them.
> 
> Each recovery agent sees only what the Recovery Pass section permits.
> 
> Return candidate records with:
> 
> * provisional candidate ID
> * candidate claim or transition
> * candidate type
> * attribution
> * source pointer
> * supporting excerpt or concise evidence description
> * origin episode
> * tension or reason the item might matter
> * uncertainty
> * no current-frame interpretation yet
> 
> Preserve source wording carefully.
> 
> Do not overcompress.
> 
> Do not score quality.
> 
> Do not reject a candidate merely because its destination is unclear.
> 
> ---
> 
> ## Stage B: Lineage analysis
> 
> Determine:
> 
> * which candidates share the same origin
> * which are derivative
> * which recur independently
> * which later receive explicit reinforcement
> * which appear to evolve across episodes
> 
> Record lineage rather than merging away useful history.
> 
> For near-identical claims from different episodes, preserve the episodes and connect them.
> 
> ---
> 
> ## Stage C: Arc reconstruction
> 
> Use a dedicated pass to inspect the calibration material chronologically.
> 
> Look for:
> 
> * ideas born and later changed
> * repeated forms of pushback
> * recurring questions
> * recurring reasoning moves
> * rejected ideas
> * principles that became stronger over time
> * provisional ideas that later disappeared
> * places where Venkat's steering materially changed the result
> * cases where an assistant-originated idea became explicitly endorsed
> * cases where later documents merely inherited earlier language
> 
> Do not invent continuity where no evidence exists.
> 
> ---
> 
> ## Stage D: Contextual interpretation
> 
> Only now introduce current governing context.
> 
> Interpret candidates against:
> 
> * DECISIONS.md
> * ROOT.md
> * VISION.md
> * DONE.md
> * TASTE.md
> * current approved identity and positioning
> * current goals and priorities
> * relevant qualified audit findings
> * current Advisor charter
> 
> For each candidate ask:
> 
> * Does this connect to a current root or governing principle?
> * Does it extend something already known?
> * Does it challenge current framing?
> * Does it reveal a lower-level reasoning move beneath a root?
> * Does it appear superseded?
> * Does it expose a contradiction?
> * Does it have no current connection yet?
> * Does it have one or more plausible future use paths?
> 
> Do not reject candidates merely because they do not fit current framing.
> 
> ---
> 
> ## Stage E: Skeptic verification
> 
> Use an evaluator separate from the extraction pass where practical.
> 
> Verify:
> 
> 1. Does the cited source exist?
> 2. Does the pointer resolve?
> 3. Does the evidence support the candidate?
> 4. Is attribution correct?
> 5. Is claimed repetition based on independent episodes?
> 6. Is a supposed pattern merely propagated text?
> 7. Is a transition supported by the chronological record?
> 8. Is private or prohibited material being exposed?
> 9. Has an implementation instruction been mistaken for an insight?
> 10. Has current positioning contaminated recovery?
> 11. Is the candidate stronger than the evidence allows?
> 
> The skeptic does not kill candidates based on taste.
> 
> Remove or quarantine only for reasons such as:
> 
> * fabricated evidence
> * broken provenance
> * wrong attribution
> * prohibited material
> * unsupported inference presented as fact
> * duplicate copy with no independent value
> * explicit hard-constraint violation
> 
> Record the reason.
> 
> ---
> 
> # Calibration review deliverable
> 
> Create one calibration review file.
> 
> Suggested location:
> 
> `plans/seed-mining-advisor-audit/`
> 
> Use an appropriate versioned filename consistent with repository conventions.
> 
> Do not write anything into `seeds/`.
> 
> Organize the review roughly as:
> 
> ## 1. Calibration sources
> 
> For each:
> 
> * file
> * why selected
> * coverage role
> 
> ## 2. His reasoning patterns
> 
> Only claim repetition when supported by distinct episodes.
> 
> For each candidate:
> 
> * candidate ID
> * claim or reasoning move
> * attribution
> * source pointers
> * independent episode count
> * lineage note
> * tension
> * current-context interpretation
> * provisional possible-use tags
> * skeptic note
> * evidence class
> 
> ## 3. Reasoning transitions
> 
> Show:
> 
> * before
> * pressure or contradiction
> * after
> * source chain
> * what caused the change if supported
> 
> ## 4. Steering-to-outcome examples
> 
> Show cases where Venkat's intervention materially changed the result.
> 
> ## 5. Endorsed ideas
> 
> Assistant or other-originated ideas later adopted by Venkat.
> 
> Keep origin explicit.
> 
> ## 6. Other-authored ideas worth preserving
> 
> Do not imply ownership.
> 
> ## 7. Build-shaped findings
> 
> Separate:
> 
> * implementation layer
> * underlying conceptual layer, if one exists
> 
> ## 8. Lineage findings
> 
> Show important examples of:
> 
> * independent recurrence
> * propagation
> * derived restatement
> * later reinforcement
> 
> ## 9. What the method appears to miss
> 
> The system should critique its own recovery.
> 
> ## 10. Calibration questions for Venkat
> 
> Do not ask broad design questions.
> 
> Surface only the judgments required to determine whether the full mine should proceed.
> 
> Examples:
> 
> * Are these the kinds of things you mean by seeds?
> * Are we recovering your reasoning rather than assistant phrasing?
> * Is the granularity too small, too large, or about right?
> * Are transitions useful enough to remain a first-class category?
> * Are source pointers sufficient for trust?
> * Are possible-use tags useful, distracting, or premature?
> 
> Keep this section concise.
> 
> ---
> 
> # Persist the revised plan
> 
> Revise the original plan before or alongside the calibration run.
> 
> If no persisted version exists yet, save the revised plan under:
> 
> `plans/seed-mining-advisor-audit/seed-mining-advisor-audit--pipeline--extraction--v1--2026-08-16-<HHmm>.md`
> 
> If a v1 plan already exists and is meant to remain frozen, create the next version rather than overwriting it.
> 
> Update `plans/INDEX.md` according to repository rules.
> 
> The revised plan must explicitly contain:
> 
> * recovery before interpretation
> * episode-level lineage
> * below-file deduplication
> * reasoning transitions
> * conceptual extraction from build-shaped material
> * no abductive-extension extraction lens
> * possible-use tags after recovery
> * calibration before full mine
> * mandatory stop after calibration review
> 
> ---
> 
> # Hard constraints
> 
> Preserve all hard constraints from the existing plan and current canon, including:
> 
> * no seed files written
> * no client, employer, or brand names in candidate seed language where repo rules prohibit them
> * D-003 identity rules
> * unresolved restricted numerical claims remain restricted
> * archived or prohibited transcript material stays excluded
> * attribution remains honest
> * open Advisor-boundary questions remain open
> * conversations remain reasoning history rather than canon
> * consequential promotion stays human-controlled
> * no external publishing or outreach
> * no deletion of source material
> * no silent canonical changes
> * no automatic promotion to ROOT, TASTE, DECISIONS, procedural memory, or seeds
> 
> If a hard constraint conflicts with this prompt, repository canon wins.
> 
> Surface the conflict rather than silently resolving it.
> 
> ---
> 
> # Method constraints
> 
> Use the least complex mechanism that preserves:
> 
> * coverage
> * provenance
> * source fidelity
> * attribution
> * lineage
> * independent-evidence counting
> * reversibility
> * inspectability
> 
> Prefer scripts for deterministic operations.
> 
> Prefer model judgment for semantic interpretation.
> 
> Do not build permanent infrastructure merely to perform this calibration.
> 
> Temporary scratch artifacts are acceptable if clearly labeled and safely removable.
> 
> Do not create a new database, graph system, registry, agent architecture, or permanent ontology merely because lineage analysis exists.
> 
> ---
> 
> # Validation and testing
> 
> Before reporting completion, validate all of the following.
> 
> ## Source coverage validation
> 
> * Every calibration source exists.
> * Every selected source is recorded in the calibration manifest.
> * Selection rationale exists.
> * No prohibited source entered the run.
> 
> ## Provenance validation
> 
> Randomly select at least 10 candidate records, or all candidates if fewer than 10.
> 
> For each verify:
> 
> * source file resolves
> * source location resolves
> * quoted or summarized evidence matches context
> * speaker is correct
> * attribution is correct
> * origin episode is recorded
> * lineage classification is defensible
> 
> Report failures.
> 
> ## Repetition validation
> 
> For every candidate described as repeated:
> 
> * verify at least two distinct origin episodes
> * verify the later instance is not merely copied or derived
> * if independence is uncertain, downgrade the claim
> 
> No candidate should earn "repeated pattern" status from document count alone.
> 
> ## Transition validation
> 
> For every reasoning-transition candidate:
> 
> * verify chronological order
> * verify both states are represented
> * verify the claimed change is supported
> * distinguish explicit change from inferred change
> 
> ## Anchoring validation
> 
> Inspect the Stage A recovery artifacts.
> 
> Verify they contain no current-positioning interpretation or Advisor-derived discovery framing that was supposed to enter only at Stage D.
> 
> If contamination occurred, rerun the affected recovery pass cleanly.
> 
> ## Attribution validation
> 
> Specifically inspect candidates derived from assistant language.
> 
> Verify:
> 
> * `other` stays other absent adoption evidence
> * `endorsed` has explicit adoption, reformulation, or approval evidence
> * `his` traces to Venkat's own reasoning
> * `system` is clearly an inference
> 
> ## Constraint validation
> 
> Confirm:
> 
> * zero files created in `seeds/`
> * no prohibited names leaked into candidate seed formulations
> * no restricted archived material was used
> * no canonical file was silently changed
> * no implementation idea was automatically promoted
> 
> ## Output validation
> 
> Confirm the review file contains:
> 
> * his thinking first
> * reasoning transitions
> * steering-to-outcome evidence
> * attribution
> * source pointers
> * lineage
> * possible-use tags
> * skeptic notes
> * uncertainty
> * calibration questions
> 
> ---
> 
> # Evaluation of the calibration
> 
> At the end, assess the mining method itself.
> 
> Use these questions:
> 
> 1. Does the method recover Venkat's reasoning rather than mostly assistant content?
> 2. Does lineage detection materially reduce false repetition?
> 3. Does independent recovery surface material that a frame-first search would miss?
> 4. Do reasoning transitions provide information static claims miss?
> 5. Are candidate units appropriately sized?
> 6. Is provenance strong enough for later seed creation?
> 7. Is the method producing too much low-value fragmentation?
> 8. Is any stage adding complexity without improving the review?
> 9. What would you remove before the full run?
> 10. What evidence supports proceeding or revising?
> 
> Do not decide on Venkat's behalf whether the calibration is good enough.
> 
> Prepare the evidence for his judgment.
> 
> ---
> 
> # Stop condition
> 
> STOP after:
> 
> 1. revised plan is persisted
> 2. plan index is updated where required
> 3. calibration tranche is complete
> 4. lineage and skeptic checks are complete
> 5. calibration review file is written
> 6. validation is complete
> 7. a concise execution receipt is produced
> 
> Do not mine the remaining corpus.
> 
> Do not create seed files.
> 
> Do not move approved-looking candidates into downstream systems.
> 
> Venkat reviews the calibration first.
> 
> ---
> 
> # Final report to Venkat
> 
> Return a concise checkpoint containing:
> 
> ## Files created
> 
> Exact paths.
> 
> ## Files modified
> 
> Exact paths and why.
> 
> ## Calibration corpus
> 
> Which sources were selected and why.
> 
> ## Results
> 
> * number of raw recovered candidates
> * number after lineage reconciliation
> * number of independent recurring patterns
> * number of reasoning transitions
> * number of steering-to-outcome examples
> * number by attribution class
> * number quarantined or removed, with reasons
> 
> ## Method findings
> 
> What worked, what failed, and what you would change before full mining.
> 
> ## Validation
> 
> List every validation performed and whether it passed.
> 
> ## Important uncertainties
> 
> Only uncertainties material to the decision about proceeding.
> 
> ## Stop confirmation
> 
> Explicitly confirm:
> 
> * full corpus mine did not run
> * no seed files were written
> * no candidate was promoted
> * no prohibited source was used
> 
> End with one decision for Venkat:
> 
> > Approve the calibrated extraction contract for the full corpus, or revise it based on the calibration evidence.

> ## Priority E — Minimum Sufficient Mechanism
> 
> **L4-CAND-19** (lines 5873-5875): Venkat states, in his own plain words: "I want to make sure that whatever output identifies or uses the simplest way possible without affecting the maximum. Grep confirms this is the *first* occurrence of any "simplest/minimum sufficient" phrasing in the raw L4 file — nothing earlier in that 6254-line conversation uses this framing. Lineage_status: **origin**, with the assistant performing formalization/naming immediately after, not independent invention.

> **Relationship to CLUSTER-06/07:** This is a **related but distinct** idea, not simply a new member of either existing cluster. CLUSTER-06 (over-building/analysis-recursion caution) is about *not letting build/analysis substitute for action*. CLUSTER-07 (manual-before-automation) is about *sequencing* — prove the shape by hand before automating. "Minimum sufficient mechanism" is a *design-selection* rule: once you've decided to build something, choose the least complex mechanism that still delivers the full required outcome. It shares the anti-overbuilding spirit of CLUSTER-06 but operates at a different altitude (mechanism choice vs. process discipline). <a id="ec41466c-7f75-4e45-85d5-0c8af89c081e"></a>
**Venkat** · `ec41466c-7f75-4e45-85d5-0c8af89c081e`

> can you just give me some instructions on how to go about reviewing the seeds and selecting them?


<a id="5ad3a92d-15cf-4421-93bb-187c878c4316"></a>
**Claude** · `5ad3a92d-15cf-4421-93bb-187c878c4316`

How to run your pass — it's smaller than it looks:

- **Open the review file, sections 1 and 7 only.** That's 13 patterns, not 882 candidates. Everything else in the file is context, not picks.
- **For each pattern, one question:** would you ask for this by name later? If yes, it's a seed. If you hesitate, cut it — cutting is cheap, the record keeps it either way.
- **Give each a one-word verdict:** keep / cut / edit. "Edit" means the idea is right but the words aren't yours — say the sentence the way you'd say it, and that becomes the seed's title.
- **Trust the labels.** "Verified yours" survived a skeptic trying to disprove it; "document-voiced" means the idea is yours but the sentence isn't — those need your wording or a cut.
- **Ignore the singletons for now.** The wide net kept 400+ small items; a second pass over those is optional, later, and only if the first batch proves useful.
- **Then hand me the verdicts** (even just "keep 1,2,5, edit 7: '…', cut the rest"). I draft each seed file, you approve, and only then anything touches `seeds/`.

Ten to fifteen minutes if you go with your gut, which is the right speed — the evidence work is already done.
